AG01/AG03 implemented: `mandate.update` joins the typed shared command boundary. Current membership/mandate access is checked; input must contain the reviewed base revision and cannot override actor/approval scope. Repository update performs gate acquisition, locked membership check, xmin compare-and-swap and successful audit in one SQL statement, preserving both PostgreSQL-driver interfaces. Existing `saveBrief` may create a mandate but refuses unversioned edits. Mandate reads/list carry an opaque revision. This is a server command, not an automatically exposed model tool or new send authority; the eventual agent mutation adapter still requires exact human approval.

Manual brief API and existing editor use this same command. Stale requests are rejected before AI regeneration; after regeneration the current mandate is read again and SQL CAS remains the final write guard. An intervening edit cannot be overwritten. UI captures the editor's original base revision rather than accepting background prop changes. Conflict retains unsaved text and stops research launch; explicit Load saved brief warns that local text is replaced and adopts the fetched revision. `briefOnly=true` reads just the authenticated saved mandate with no-store and bypasses enrichment/research recovery. No model is invoked by reload. Component tests verify background changes, preserved edits and successful explicit reload/retry. API and command tests cover stale/missing revisions and mid-generation conflict. Mutation failure responses do not expose SQL or parameters.

RED/GREEN evidence: new shared command rejected its unknown name and real-PG concurrency test first failed for the missing revision; subsequent actual gate requirement was exposed and fixed by acquiring the installed protocol gate before row locks. New UI tests failed for missing base revision and reload control, then passed. Mid-regeneration tests failed by accepting an obsolete update, then passed after command precheck. Real-PG verifies exactly one concurrent winner, one audit, foreign/nonmember denial and membership revocation while an update waits on the global fence. The last case initially exposed a local clone issue, rather than an application CAS failure: `communication_member_fence` contained no singleton, so its statement trigger could not record transaction ownership.

User requested lower idle database polling while retaining fast testing and bounded ready-work processing. Normal mode now waits30seconds after the first empty check and60seconds on continued idle, resets idle backoff when work appears, and checks again after1second following productive work. A burst starts at most5sequential ticks and starts no new tick after10seconds; in-flight ticks retain the original55second HTTP timeout (the10seconds is a start budget, not forced cancellation). Review/defer/lost outcomes end their burst. Failure retries back off1/2/4/8/16/32/60seconds. Research maintenance remains independent, once-minute and non-overlapping. No browser flag or new permission can select execution authority. Cadence logs contain mode/delay/batch metadata only on changes; empty polls do not generate repeated logs.
